## Weapon: Modular Turret Mount
### Data Extract Profile
[[File:BA - Modular Turret Mount.png|thumb|right|Modular Turret Mount]]

==Description==
The '''Modular Turret Mount''' (MTM) is a piece of [[battle armor]] equipment that allows the operator to swap weapons based on mission parameters.  Based on the [[Modular Weapon Mount]], the MTM is designed to be used by quadruped battle armor designs and mount multiple weapons on a 360° swivel assembly.  Turret mounts actually come in two varieties: the standard hard-mount turret designed to carry a set amount of weight, and configurable turret which can carry a wider range of equipment but is bulkier and weighs more.<ref name="TMp263">''TechManual'', p. 263, "Modular Turret Mount"</ref>

==Notes==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Game Notes ===
Only quadruped battlesuits may carry Modular Turret Mounts and may carry only one.  Standard turret mounts take up one space slot and may weigh between 40 kg to 130 kg with between one to ten additional space slots for mounting equipment.  Configurable turret mounts take up two space slots and may weigh between 50 kg to 140 kg with between one to nine additional space slots for mounting equipment.<ref name="TMp263"/>
</div>

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==
* ''[[TechManual]]''

## Weapon: Modular Weapon Mount
### Data Extract Profile
{{rewrite|something went wrong here; info was cited to TechManual not found in TechManual, in a section that was under the wrong name}}
[[File:ModularWeaponMount.jpg|right|200px|thumb|Modular Weapon Mount System]]

==Description==
The '''Modular Weapon Mount''' is a piece of [[battle armor]] equipment, [[Clan]] and Inner Sphere, that allows the operator to swap weapons based on mission parameters. Engineers were able to reverse engineer the technology from the [[Clan]] [[Elemental (Battle Armor)|Elemental]] battlesuit.{{cn}} Prior to the Modular Weapon Mount Inner Sphere battle armor was restricted to [[Armored Glove]]s (to use conventional infantry weapons) or fixed weapons (used on suits like the [[Infiltrator]] and [[Sloth]]).

Weighing 10kg each<ref name="TMp262">''TechManual'', p. 262: "Modular/Turret Mounts"</ref>, the Modular Weapon Mount has harnesses, weapon status lines, and targeting interfaces for a variety of battlesuit primary weapon systems. A suit of battle armor equipped with MWMs can mount any weapon system as long as the armor has the space and weight available. Though the armor equipped with Modular Weapon Mounts cannot use them to exceed the normal weight restrictions, the flexibility offered is worth the minor weight penalty. Battle Armor suits can mount two MWM on the body and one on each arm. Quad battlesuits use the [[Modular Turret Mount]] instead of the standard Modular Weapon Mount.<ref name="TMp262"/>

The Modular Weapon Mount was also used as the basis for the [[Anti-Personnel Weapon Mount]], [[Modular Turret Mount]], and [[Squad Support Weapon Mount]].

## Weapon: Naval C3 System
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
An outgrowth of the [[Draconis Combine|Combine]]'s C3 systems, the Naval C3 was introduced in [[3065]], this conceptual supernetwork promises to link [[WarShip]] squadrons into finely tuned flotillas with greater coordination and firepower than any equivalent aerospace unit. Combine researchers swiftly found the act of constructing such a large and effective coordination network—capable of linking fire control for massive WarShip-mounted batteries—a lot more daunting than it was for ground forces, however. Adding in the unusual time delays of aerospace weapons fire and sensor readings presented additional challenges that nearly scrapped the project. Nevertheless, the [[Nirasaki Computers Collective]] persevered and was fielding functional, if limited, prototypes in the late 3060s. Regrettably, with few Combine WarShips and [[DropShips]] prepared to fully benefit from the technology, the disruption wreaked by the [[Jihad]] on the Combine’s shipyards left wide deployment of the naval C3 combat system in limbo.<ref name="TOp144">''Tactical Operations'', p. 144, "Naval C3 System"</ref> 

==Notes==
* Rules can be found in ''[[Tactical Operations]]''<ref name="TOp144">''Tactical Operations'', p. 144, "Naval C3 System"</ref>

## Weapon: Naval Comm-Scanner Suite
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
A '''Naval Comm-Scanner Suite''' is a set of multispectral telescopes and processing units first invented for [[WarShip]]s by the [[Terran Alliance]] circa [[2200]].<ref name="TOp331-332">''Tactical Operations'', pp. 331-332, "Naval Comm-Scanner Suite"</ref>  It was primarily used to monitor communications and detect spacecraft emissions with much greater accuracy than that provided by standard sensor suites.  Popular myth held that an NCSS could listen in on spoken conversations aboard another vessel, though in truth a covert signaling device was required for these operations.  The NCSS was most famously used by the [[Star League]]'s ''[[Bug-Eye]]'' surveillance ships.<ref name="TOp331-332"/>

## Weapon: Neutrino Detector
### Data Extract Profile
A '''neutrino detector''' is a sensor system capable of detecting and identifying the neutrino emissions produced by [[fusion engine]]s or nuclear reactors. Being effectively impossible to shield against, neutrino detectors represented one of the most powerful sensor systems developed during the [[Star League]] era.<ref name=AT2REp110>''AeroTech 2 Revised Edition'', p. 110: "Naval Warfare: Detection"</ref><ref name=SOp248>''Strategic Operations'', p. 248: "Aerospace Operations: Detection"</ref>

==Overview==
Most [[Inner Sphere]] and [[Clan]] fusion power plants use hydrogen as fuel for thermonuclear fusion. This reaction produces [[w:Neutrino|neutrinos]] as a byproduct. These near-massless particles interact with matter exceptionally rarely, making neutrino emissions effectively impossible to conceal, but also makes them exceptionally difficult to detect. Despite this difficulty, the Star League produced specialized neutrino detector "tanks" that are capable of detecting the neutrino emissions.<ref name=AT2REp122>''AeroTech 2 Revised Edition'', p. 122: "Neutrino Signatures"</ref>

Neutrino detectors are enormous systems and were generally installed only aboard [[WarShip]]s of heavy-cruiser size or larger, whose mass allowed them to spare the thousands of tons required for the system. Even during the Star League, they were fitted to only a small percentage of these large WarShips due to their cost.<ref name=SOp248/><ref name=AT2REp122/>

==Operation==
Following arrival in a new system, a neutrino detector must be recalibrated. The crew must first account for the neutrinos released by the system's star and the detector vessel's own fusion drive.<ref name=AT2REp122/> This process requires between two and twelve hours.<ref name=ECp84>''Explorer Corps'', p. 84: "Neutrino Detectors"</ref><ref name=SOp248/>

Once calibrated, the detector can scan for neutrino sources at ranges of up to four AU.<ref name=ECp84/> Unlike many conventional sensors, planetary bodies and other physical obstacles provide effectively no concealment,<ref name=AT2REp110/> allowing vessels to be detected even when hidden behind a planet.

The neutrino emissions of individual vessels are unique, with variations resulting from the vessel's class and age. After six hours of uninterrupted tracking, a detector can develop a profile sufficient to identify the vessel's class. After twelve hours, the profile becomes sufficiently detailed to positively identify that specific vessel during later encounters, creating a "fingerprint" for the vessel.<ref name=ECp84/><ref name=SOp248/>

==Countermeasures==
Because neutrinos interact so weakly with ordinary matter, they can pass through enormous quantities of material. Consequently, no countermeasures exist to shield an operating reactor from a neutrino detector.<ref name=AT2REp110/> The only effective means identified for hiding a neutrino signature is to shut the reactor down<ref name=SOp248/>  or to leave the detector's range.

==Availability==
By the thirty-first century, functioning neutrino detectors were exceptionally rare. The [[Clans]] were believed to retain a small number aboard surviving Star League-era vessels.<ref name=SOp248/>

[[ComStar]] and the [[Word of Blake]] were also believed to possess the technology through their inheritance of [[Terra]], but contemporary military speculation held that neither organization had restarted production. The [[Explorer Corps]] did not possess a working neutrino-detection system for its vessels.<ref name=ECp44>''Explorer Corps'', p. 44: "Neutrino Signatures"</ref> The Word of Blake may have possessed a handful of mobile detectors at the beginning of the [[Jihad]], though its heavy WarShip losses were believed to have reduced this inventory to only planetary detector installations on Terra.<ref name=SOp248/>

==Notes==
* {{asof|2026}}, there are no current rules for neutrino detectors; the rules were not brought forward from ''[[AeroTech 2 Revised Edition]]'' and ''[[Explorer Corps (sourcebook)|Explorer Corps]]''. According to [[Mike Miller]] in [https://www.battletech.com/forums/index.php/topic,85741.msg2024980.html a 2024 Ask the Writers answer], while the equipment is discussed in ''[[Strategic Operations]]'', rules for neutrino detectors were left out of the ''[[Total Warfare]]''-era rulebooks as they would effectively eliminate stealth in space.

## Weapon: Nova Combined Electronic Warfare System
### Data Extract Profile
Appearing briefly during the [[Wars of Reaving]], the '''Nova Combined Electronic Warfare System''' (also known as '''Nova CEWS''') was developed by [[The Society]] to offset their warriors' inexperience in comparison to more seasoned [[Clan]] warriors.<ref name=TWoRp203>''The Wars of Reaving'', p. 203, "Rules Annex - Nova CEWS"</ref> <ref name=TWoRSp18>''The Wars of Reaving Supplemental'', p. 18, "Tools Of Blood"</ref>

==Description==
The rogue scientists of the Society developed the Nova CEWS in [[3065]] after hearing about the [[C3 network|C3 systems]] used by the [[Inner Sphere]]. By combining elements of the [[Clan Smoke Jaguar]]-developed [[Watchdog Combined Electronic Warfare System]] with a [[Improved C3 Computer]]-style system, they created a tool that could find enemy forces, hide the unit using it in an ECM bubble, and automatically provide targeting data to other friendly units. In addition, the Nova CEWS didn't require preconfiguration for the C3 capability; It could be programmed on the fly to respond to availability of friendly forces. The advantages of the Nova CEWS would prove highly useful to close gap between the Society's more green and inexperienced pilot corps and elite though overconfident Clan warriors.<ref name=TWoRp203/>

Despite these advances, the system did have some notable limitations. The probe and ECM range remained the same as the Watchdog's, the highly compact components of the system generate heat, and the system's network was limited to a total of three nodes. The limitation of network size in part drove the Society's adoption of its [[Society Military Structure|Prime military structure]] of Septs and Treys. The system can also only be mounted on [[BattleMech]]s, [[Aerospace Fighter]]s, [[Small Craft]] and [[fusion engine|fusion-powered]] [[Combat Vehicle]]s; units smaller than a 'Mech such as [[ProtoMech]]s and [[Battle Armor]] or larger than a Small Craft such as [[DropShip]]s, [[JumpShip]]s and [[WarShip]]s are unable to be connected to Nova CEWS network. The Nova CEWS is only useful for ground units and is effectively non-viable in space-based combat.<ref name=TWoRp203/>

After the end of the Wars of Reaving [[Clan Stone Lion]] was given permission to experiment with the few Nova CEWS remaining, but only if they used the system in ''[[solahma]]'' and second line [[combat vehicle]] [[Point]]s facing non-Clan opponents. The Nova CEWS was banned for use in 'Mech designs.<ref name=TWoRSp18/> <ref name=TWoRSp16>''The Wars of Reaving Supplemental'', p. 16, "Tactics of War - Clan Stone Lion"</ref>

==References==

* ''[[The Wars of Reaving Supplemental]]''

## Weapon: Paramedic Equipment
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
'''Paramedic Equipment''' refers to emergency trauma gear more extensive than found in a typical [[Medical Kit|first aid kit]], consisting of defibrillators, chemical stimulates and tranquilizers, [[Preserving Sleeve|preserving sleeves]], and other medical instruments.  In the hands of a [[Field Medic]] this gear can save a person's life, which is why it is standard issue on [[Support Vehicle]]s such as ambulances and rescue vehicles.  It is also commonly found on dedicated surgical units like [[MASH Truck]]s to allow for a triage station to be set up.  Very rarely is this equipment found in dedicated combat units, since most soldiers are taught only the basics of first aid.<ref name="TMp233">''TechManual'', p. 233, "Paramedic Equipment"</ref>

== Rules ==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Game Rules ===
Paramedic Equipment may be mounted on any unit in 0.25 Ton allocations in accordance with standard construction rules except [[ProtoMech]]s.<ref name="TMp233"/>

Each instance of the equipment costs 7,500 C-Bills. The [[Availability Rating]] is C-C-C.<ref name=TM293>''TechManual'', pp. 293, "Industrial Equipment"</ref> 
</div>

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==

## Weapon: Partial Wing (BattleMech)
### Data Extract Profile
[[Image:Cougar-XR.jpg|210px|right|thumb|Experimental ''Cougar-XR'' equipped with a partial wing system]]

==Description==
First designed by [[Clan Jade Falcon]] in [[3061]] and entering production in [[3067]], the partial wing system offers increased jump capability comparable to the [[improved jump jet]] system, but also increases the rate of heat dissipation. Not to be confused with the [[Land-Air 'Mech]] concept, the partial wing is a fixed-wing system that allows a 'Mech to glide an additional 60 meters when using normal [[jump jet]]s. In addition, its large surface area allows a 'Mech to more efficiently radiate excess heat. The technology was later adapted for use in [[ProtoMechs]] by [[Clan Blood Spirit]] starting in [[3063]] and entering production by [[3070]].<ref name=TOp293>''Tactical Operations'', p. 293: "BattleMech/ProtoMech Partial Wing"</ref>

==Notes==
* Rules related information for the Partial Wing can be found on ''Tactical Operations'', p. 293; ''Interstellar Operations'', pp. 100–102; ''Experimental Technical Readout: The Clans'', p. 3; and ''Technical Readout: Prototypes''. p. 206.

## Weapon: Pintle Mount
### Data Extract Profile
[[File:Pintle Vehicle modified.jpg|right|thumb|Highlighted Pintle Mount fitted on a '''[[Pintel Heavy Combat ATV]]''']]

==Description==
'''Pintle Mount''' is a powered yet unarmored turret that can be mounted on a [[combat vehicle|combat]], ground, or even air type [[support vehicle]]. This mount is intended to allow [[Infantry|infantryman]] to use heavy man-portable weapons up to a half ton in weight. The mount has a limited arc of 180° depending on the vehicle's direction. Due to the limited firing arc, this is rarely seen mounted on Combat Vehicles. The Pintle Mount has existed since the Pre-spaceflight Era.<ref name=TM235>''TechManual'', pp. 235, 344: "Pintle Mount" - Everything regarding the Pintle Mount, including construction and game rules for using it</ref>

== Rules ==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Construction Rules ===
* '''Availability''' - Clan and Inner Sphere and has a '''Tech Rating''' of A.<ref name=TM235/>

*Pintle Mounts are only able to be equipped with weapons no heavier than 500 kg. They cannot be fitted with any armor protection and its weight must be equal to five percent of the equipment and weapons mounted on it excluding ammunition rounded up per type of vehicle.{{cn}}  For small Support Vehicles, the weight is rounded up to the nearest kilogram, while for combat vehicles it rounded up to closest half-ton.  It should be noted only the weapons mounted on the Pintle Mount occupies a critical slot, while pintle mount does not.<ref name=TM235/>

* '''Units Restrictions''': Pintle Mounts are not allowed on Fixed-Wing Support Aircraft, Naval Craft.<ref name=TM235/>

=== Game Rules ===
* '''Firing Arc''' - The equipment can be aimed throughout a 180° arc.  When the Pintle Mount is fitted to the front or back of a vehicle, it has the same firing arc as a BattleMech’s Front arc, with the same 3 hex facing. Should it be mounted aft, it would also have 3-hex facing, like the front.  When mounted on the sides, the Pintle Mount has the same firing arcs Fore- and Aft-Side arcs that a Spheroid shaped [[DropShip]] has. It should be remembered that the mount is limited to the side where it's placed.<ref name=TM235/>
* '''Requirements''' - The vehicle that the Pintle Mount is fitted on must be powered and its designated gunnery crew must be active. If the crew is killed or stunned, they will not be able to use it.<ref name=TM235/>
</div>

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==

## Weapon: Recon Camera
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
A '''Recon Camera''' is a battlefield surveillance camera, though its use in the [[thirty-first century]] has declined.  Technically a group of visual sensors, a Recon Camera can provide high-resolution holographic data used to map terrain and locate enemy units.  Recon Cameras are typically mounted on low-tech platforms, such as the [[Boomerang]] or civilian newsvid VTOLs.<ref name="TOp337-338">''Tactical Operations'', pp. 337-338, "Recon Camera"</ref>

== Rules ==
<div class="gamerules">

=== Game Rules ===
The Recon Camera has a maximum range of eighteen kilometers and can only be used on the Low Altitude map or ground map.  Units equipped with a Recon Camera may act as a spotter for [[LRM]] Indirect Fire as if it were a [[Target Acquisition Gear|TAG]], but cannot
use it to designate targets for other TAG-guided weapons, such as [[Semi-Guided LRM|semi-guided LRMs]], [[Laser-Guided Bomb|laser-guided bombs]] and [[Arrow IV Homing Missile|Arrow IV artillery missiles]].  Hidden units that are passed over by a Recon Camera-equipped flier must reveal themselves on an unsuccessful 2d6 roll.<ref name="TOp337-338"/>
</div>

==References==
<references/>

*''[[Tactical Operations]]''

## Weapon: Remote Sensor Dispenser
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
'''Remote Sensor Dispensers''' are small cargo bays with an attached dispenser used to place [[Remote Sensor]]s.  These dispensers are normally mounted on [[support vehicle]]s, used to secure newly-established landing zones or defensive perimeters.  Each RSD can carry up to thirty short-range sensors, typically a mix of thermal, electromagnetic and seismic sensors.<ref name="TMp236">''TechManual'', p. 236, "Remote Sensor Dispenser"</ref> 

<div class="gamerules">
== Game Rules ==
Remote Sensor Dispensers may be mounted on and deployed by any ground or naval vehicle, [[BattleMech]]s, and any air unit capable of flying within 7 Altitudes of the ground.  Additional ammo may be added at a rate of 30 sensors per half ton.  Remote Sensors may be deployed at the cost of 1 MP and follow the standard rules per [[Tactical Operations]].<ref name="TOp337">''Tactical Operations'', p. 375, "Remote Sensors"</ref>
</div>

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==

*''[[TechManual]]''

## Weapon: Salvage Arm
### Data Extract Profile
The '''Salvage Arm''' is a noncombat system which is used for purposes of handing ammunition, repairs, and salvage.  The arm itself has a highly dexterous, light manipulator which is not reinforced for physical attacks. 

There are two types of Salvage Arms: [['Mech]] scale and suit scale.
  
It was originally developed strictly to be used on [[IndustrialMechs]] and it can be used on BattleMechs.  It was later developed into being used on smaller industrial robotic suits such as [[Exoskeleton|Exoskeletons]].

==History==
The ''Salvage Arm'' is ancient technology, where the technology was first developed circa the year [[2000]]. 
It was developed further for use with exoskeletons in [[2415]], by the [[Terran Hegemony]].

== Capabilities ==
=== 'Mech scale Salvage Arm ===
The Salvage Arm can lift cargo for short periods of time, but it is not designed for long-term lifting cargo like the [[Lift hoist]] or Hand Actuators.   

It can be used to load ammunition into various vehicles, BattleMechs, and other equipment.

=== Suit-scale Salvage Arm ===
This is a multitool device found on Industrial grade exoskeletons and battlefield recovery suits.  The arm has a clamp-like hand/manipulator and cutting tools.  This device is noted as being useless in combat situations, but excels in salvage and recovery.

== Models ==
===DiNapoli ST7===
{|class="wikitable" style="text-align:center; border-spacing: 2px; border: 2px solid black;"
|'''Manufactured on'''  
|'''Manufactured by'''
|'''Typically used in'''

|----

|}

==Notes==
* In ''[[TechManual]]'', the article for Salvage Arm notes further uses for the salvage arm would be detailed in the then-upcoming ''[[Tactical Operations]]'' manual, which contains Advanced rules.  However, other than the entry above, no entry in that manual features any further function for the salvage arm.

== Rules ==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Game Rules ===
'Mech scale salvage arms may only be used on BattleMechs and IndustrialMechs. Exoskeleton manipulator type size salvage arms can be used on all types of biped 'suits.

In Standard Game rules, it serves no purpose. However, it can be used in when conducting physical combat when need be.  When used, it is treated as having no hand actuator, subjected to the penalties of a handless arm.

When using the optional rules found in the Tactical Operations book, the arm can be used to assist in loading ammunition while under combat conditions during a game

When equipping a 'Mech to have a salvage arm, the 'Mech's hand actuator is replaced by it.

Suit-sized salvage arms remain operational as long as the suit is not destroyed or the trooper is not killed.
</div>

## Weapon: Satellite Uplink
### Data Extract Profile
'''Satellite Uplink''' equipment is a form of mobile technology enabling advanced tactical communications to equipped [[BattleMech]]s.

==Description==
Many [['Mechs]] and vehicles have unique or advanced communications systems, but these normally have little impact on the battlefield. The enhanced '''Satellite Uplink''' system carried by such 'Mechs as the ''[[Cyclops]]'' and ''[[Atlas]]'' is an exception. This advanced communications system gives the equipped unit an excellent view of the battlefield for all units on its side, but cannot coordinate closely with its fellow units while its monitors are dedicated to satellite comms.<ref name="MTp59>''Maximum Tech'', p. 59: "Satellite Uplink"</ref>

## Weapon: Searchlight
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
A '''Searchlight''' is a piece of equipment for illumination which comes in a broad range of sizes. Smaller handheld devices can pump out a few hundred watts to produce illumination up to 270 meters and are light enough to be mounted on [[battle armor]] and [[exoskeleton]]s.<ref name="TMp269">''TechManual'', p. 269: "Searchlight"</ref> Heavier and larger searchlights mounted on vehicles and [[Mech]]s can light up targets as far away as five kilometers with thousands of watts of illumination.<ref name="TMp237"/>

== Rules ==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Game Rules ===
==== Normal Gameplay ====
In normal ''Total Warfare'' rules, the Searchlight does not have any effect in gameplay.<ref name="TMp237">''TechManual'', p. 237: "Searchlight"</ref>

==== Advanced Gameplay ====
When using [[Advanced Rules]] found in ''Tactical Operations'', searchlights illuminate all units in the target hex and in the intervening hexes, as well as illuminating the searchlight unit itself.  Handheld searchlights have a range of 10 hexes while mounted searchlights have a range of 30.  In scenarios which have the nighttime conditions Moonless Night, Full Moon Night or Pitch Black, units with either handheld or mounted searchlights either eliminate or lessen the to-hit modifiers.  Searchlights may also be turned on or off during either the Movement or End Phase.

If all players agree, or the scenario designates it, 'Mechs and vehicles normally not equipped with a mounted searchlight may carry one for the duration of the scenario.  Whenever said 'Mech takes a hit to the front or rear of its torso, or vehicle takes a hit in the front and sides, the player must roll a 2d6 to determine if the searchlight is destroyed.<ref name="TOp57">''Tactical Operations'', p. 57: "Weather Conditions" - Searchlight</ref>

=== Construction Rules ===
Except for [[Combat Vehicle]]s & [[BattleMechs]] all other vehicles are assumed to have three handheld-type searchlights - two in front, one in back - representing running lights, headlights and taillights or, if so designated, as strobes for emergency/police vehicles and warning strobes.  In addition, combat vehicles and 'Mechs may have a larger mounted searchlight at no cost in weight, slots or C-bills.  Additional mounted searchlights adhere to standard core construction rules, and for both vehicles and 'Mechs weigh 0.5 tons and take up one critical slot in 'Mechs, combat and [[support vehicle]]s.<ref name="TMp237"/>

Battle armor and exoskeletons may mount multiple handheld searchlights, which weigh 5 kg and take up one space slot.<ref name="TMp348">''TechManual'', p. 348: "Clan Battle Armor Equipment Table"</ref>
</div>

==Description==
Introduced in the late [[thirty-first century]] on [[Solaris VII]], the '''BattleMech Shield''' (often shortened to simply '''Shield''') is a movable piece of armor that can be mounted on BattleMech and [[IndustrialMech]] arm actuators. The shield was first introduced as a method to add protection for a MechWarrior fighting in the arenas of Solaris.  Shields are primary defensive equipment used to protect a 'Mech from being hit by weapons and some forms of physical attacks.

Initial development started in [[3065]] in the [[Lyran Alliance]]. The shield did not go beyond the [[Experimental Technology|experimental stage]] of development until after the [[Jihad]] due to the [[Word of Blake]]'s destructive occupation of Solaris VII. Once the Jihad had ended, Solaris weapons developers would move ahead with Shield Equipment in [[3079]] and produced it in limited numbers as an option for BattleMech manufacturers.

Coming in three standard sizes, a shield allows a MechWarrior to redirect damage from certain parts of the 'Mech to their shield. A 'Mech's computer can be adjusted to have the shield provide various degrees of active defensive protection. While fully active, the shield will prevent any sections it protects from firing its arm- or torso-mounted weapons. This leaves only the opposite torso, opposite arm, center torso rear, and opposite leg to fire any mounted weapons.<ref name=TOp2901>''Tactical Operations'', pp. 288, 290–291: "Shield" - Description and Construction Info</ref> 

==Notes==
The BattleMech Shield originally was published in the ''MapPack: Solaris VII'' sourcebook. It was republished in ''Tactical Operations''.

==Description==

'''Sponson turrets''' are pieces of equipment that are available to both combat vehicles and support vehicles. Often though not always mounted in pairs with one on either side of a vehicle, the sponsons allow for the placement of even larger weapons in small turrets that can protect the sides of a vehicle. This allows a vehicle to fire its side mounted weapons in a 180-degree arc, instead of the usual 60-degree arc.

While a pre-spaceflight innovation, sponson turrets were an experimental technology until [[3079]], after which they began to be mass produced and deployed by various powers. <ref name=TRO:PTp206>''Technical Readout: Prototypes'', p. 206, "Jihad Technology Advancement Table/Turrets"</ref>

## Weapon: Squad Support Weapon Mount
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
The '''Squad Support Weapon Mount''' is a piece of equipment which adopts for [[battle armor]] [[squad]]s a concept usually found in conventional [[infantry]] [[platoon]]s.  The practice of carrying a squad support weapon is more prevalent among lighter-weight battlesuit squads, allowing them to carry as a whole larger, more destructive weapons than they could carry individually; a typical example would involve a [[Kage]] squad carrying a [[Light Target Acquisition Gear (battle armor)|Light TAG]] to mark a target for artillery fire.  One trooper in the squad is designated as the squad support weapon's operator and carries the primary components of the weapon itself, while the rest of the squad carries all of the removable parts. Once the squad is in position the weapon is assembled in place and used to provide fire support.<ref name=TM263>''TechManual'', p. 263</ref><ref name="TMp270">''TechManual'', p. 270 "Light (TAG)"</ref>

==Notes==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Game Notes ===
A battle armor suit may have only one squad support weapon mount and each suit in a squad must allocate a certain percentage of the desired weapon's weight (50 for [[Inner Sphere]], 40 for [[Clan]], rounded to nearest kilogram) for the mount.  The squad's suits must also have sufficient space slots in a single location to mount the weapon and its ammunition.<ref name=TMp270/>
</div>

## Weapon: Targeting-Tracking System
### Data Extract Profile
{{about|the fire-control system of combat units|the various infantry-portable devices|Remote Sensor}}
{{TOC|align=right}}

==Description==
The '''Targeting-Tracking System''' ('''T&T'''), also known as '''Advanced Fire Control System''' (sometimes colloquially referred to as '''Sensors'''), is the system that provides sensory and targeting information for a combat unit. Introduced circa [[2439]],<ref name=TM217>''TechManual'', pp. 217–218</ref> all modern combat units ([[BattleMechs]], [[combat vehicle]]s, [[aerospace fighter]]s, [[battle armor]], etc.) include some form of T&T as part of their basic sensor suite.

On BattleMechs, the T&T incorporates a variety of sophisticated sensors with dedicated computers to process their data.  Primary amongst these are thermal imaging, light amplification, radar and magnetic anomaly sensors.  These are supplemented by secondary sensors, including motion detectors, chemical sniffers and others, all of which is interpreted and presented to the [[MechWarrior (pilot)|MechWarrior]] in an easy-to-understand format, whether on their [[cockpit]] displays or [[neurohelmet]] heads-up display.  The T&T is capable of telling friend from foe and classifying other units, even speculating on the exact variant.  It is also capable of sharing basic sensory data with another unit, though not to the same degree as available with [[C3|Command, Control and Communications (C<sup>3</sup>) systems]], and the sensory information can be saved via [[BattleROM]] for later viewing.

During the destruction of the [[Succession Wars (History)|Succession Wars]], some of the advanced fire control technology common during the [[Star League]] became [[lostech]], greatly affecting the way battles were fought.  Many systems capable of great precision over long distances could no longer be built or maintained, and often had to be replaced with more primitive examples.  Towards the end of the era, many 'Mechs had T&T systems which themselves were centuries old, often cobbled together with parts from different machines just so they could work.  Even with weapons whose theoretical maximum range was the horizon, their effective range might be greatly reduced depending on the type of target at which the 'Mech was shooting.  For example, at a distance of over 800 meters, a ''[[Shadow Hawk]]'' might have trouble landing accurate hits on another 'Mech with its [[autocannon]], but would have no issue with a stationary tower.  This technological decline also affected aerospace combat, though it be fought on a much grander scale: enemy craft might engage each other at distances of up to tens of thousands of kilometers, but the accuracy of their shots would be based on their computers' ability to predict where the enemy would be and have their fire intersect that point, and so the more evasive an opponent was the harder they were to destroy.  Ground-bound aerospace units found their fire control systems impacted by the same factors which limited 'Mech systems, greatly reducing their weapons' range.

As [[IndustrialMechs]] and [[Support Vehicles]] are designed primarily for noncombat use, they usually do not include an Advanced Fire Control System, although they can be modified.  IndustrialMechs come standard with what is known as a '''Basic Fire Control System''', which dates back to before the introduction of T&T.  While sophisticated systems for their day and still sufficient for combat, they are greatly affected by the intrinsic electronic warfare capabilities of [[thirty-first century]] combat units and are incompatible with peripheral targeting systems.  Changing out a Basic Fire Control System for an Advanced Fire Control System amounts to a simple swap and is easily accomplished, although only done so in the rare cases where an IndustrialMech is actually armed.  Support vehicles do not come standard with any FCS, and while they can be modified or built with either the basic or advanced system they do so less efficiently.<ref name=TM217/>

==Combat==
Having Advanced Fire Control system provides no bonus to the hit probability of weapons mounted on any unit.
Having Basic Fire Control systems impose a +1 to-hit penalty and to the Gunnery Skill of the unit.
Having no fire control system imposes a +2 to-hit penalty and to the Gunnery Skill of the unit.

==Models==
'''Targeting and Tracking Systems''', '''Advanced Fire Control Systems''', and '''Basic Fire Control Systems''' have been used for manufacturing on the following planets:

## Weapon: Transport Bay
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
A '''Transport Bay''' is any internal space aboard a military or civilian vehicle devoted to short- or long-term human habitation or cargo. Generically known as "bays", these storage spaces are used to carry vehicles, personnel or cargo aboard larger vehicles or spacecraft. While almost all unit types allocate some amount of internal space to transport bays of one sort or another, [[BattleMechs]] and [[ProtoMech]]s are the exception to this rule, with the effort of incorporating transport bays into their design seen as a waste of weight. Despite this, there are rumors that some forces, especially [[Clan Hell's Horses]], have attempted to create so-called "walking APCs" though this is pure speculation.<ref name="TMp239-240">''TechManual'', pp. 239-240, "Transport Bay"</ref>

=== Cargo Bay ===
'''Cargo Bays''' are found on nearly every type of unit from [[Combat Vehicle]]s to [[DropShip]]s and larger spacecraft. Most are simply open space within the unit's framework, with just clamps and other anchor points for retention straps, while more specialized variants include fixed-cubicle style "container" bays, refrigerated, insulated, liquid and even livestock storage bays.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
<div class="gamerules">

==== Rules ====
Container Cargo Bays weigh 10 tons and take up one space slot/provide 10 tons of carrying capacity per container. All other Cargo Bays have a variable weight and either take up one space slot per ton for 'Mechs or one total on vehicles; their capacity is dependent on the type of cargo they are designed to carry.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
</div>

=== Infantry bay/compartment ===
'''Infantry bays''' are typically found on large vehicles and vessels such as [[DropShips]]. They provide basic sleeping arrangements, a communal waste facility and, in some cases, stowage compartments for food and equipment. Life support is either drawn in from the outside environment through air filters and conditioners or the ship-wide life support system.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>

'''Infantry compartments''' differ in that they are meant to transport [[infantry]] over short distances and are typically found on smaller vehicles. Beyond space needed to carry soldiers and their gear to where they are going, compartments lack any of the amenities found in the larger bays.<ref name="TMp239-240"/> They are designed for deployment, not for prolonged travel.

<div class="gamerules">
==== Rules ====
All infantry bays and compartments take up either one space slot on vehicles or one slot per ton on 'Mechs. Each bay has a capacity of 28 troopers, except for mechanized infantry (which carries five troopers) and [[battle armor]] (which carries one). Foot infantry bays weigh 3 tons; jump infantry bays weigh 4 tons; motorized infantry bays weigh 5 tons; mechanized infantry bays weigh 5 tons; battle armor bays weigh 1 ton per suit (regardless of suit tonnage). All infantry bays can be combined into a single, unified infantry bay slot, regardless of tonnage or number of troopers, when used on combat or support vehicles. Infantry compartments can be of any size, fitting within one space, holding one troop for each ton.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>

Under the Advanced Rules for battle armor, four PA(L) troopers or two light battle armor troopers can be accommodated in a one-ton battle armor bay. Medium suits use the standard rules, while heavy and assault suits require 1.5 tons and two tons of space, respectively.  
</div>

=== BattleMech/Fighter Bay ===
'''BattleMech/fighter bays''' consist of one or more cubicles for transporting 'Mechs, [[aerospace fighter|aerospace]], or [[conventional aircraft]] of up to 100 tons over long distances.  Each component cubicle in these bays includes the maintenance and launch facilities for a single 'Mech or craft, along with basic living accommodations for the unit's crew and technical support personnel.  These bays are commonly found on DropShips or very large support vehicles.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>

On DropShips, each 'Mech bay cubicle includes a miniature gantry to allow technicians to work on the 'Mech along with fuel and oxygen hookups.  The open area of the bay will include additional repair facilities, storage space for spare parts, and anchor points to which a 'Mech can be secured, whether for repairs or storage during transit.  Another common feature is an airlock to allow for combat drops to be made, whether unassisted or through the use of [[Drop Cocoon]]s.<ref name=BSS20>''BattleSpace - Sourcebook'', p. 18-20</ref><ref name=AT69>''AeroTech 2'', p. 66-69</ref>

Fighter bays have a similar layout but with increased size to make landings easier for pilots and a crane for loading and unloading fighters.  Tradition also sees these bays referred to as "flight decks" and the support personnel assigned to them as the deck crew.  The bulkheads around each bay are as thick as the DropShip hull, and catch-nets can prevent out-of-control fighters from crashing into the walls.  However most fighter bays are not meant to be used in an atmosphere or when a DropShip is grounded, as they are designed around the fighter "dropping away" while the vessel is in motion.  A DropShip flying in an atmosphere can launch fighters at great risk, but it cannot recover them the same way.<ref name=BSS20/><ref name=AT69/>

<div class="gamerules">

==== Rules ====
Each 'Mech and Fighter Bay takes up one space slot, weighs 150 tons, and has a capacity of one 'Mech or craft of up to 100 tons per Cubicle.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
</div>

=== ProtoMech Bay ===
'''ProtoMech Bays''' are similar to 'Mech bays, except each cubicle can carry a full [[Clan]] [[Point]] of five [[ProtoMech]]s each. They are common only on Clan DropShips and similarly large vehicles.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
<div class="gamerules">

==== Rules ====
ProtoMech Bays take up one space slot, weigh 50 tons and have a capacity of 5 ProtoMechs per 5 Cubicles.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
</div>

=== Small Craft Bay ===
'''Small Craft Bays''' are designed for the long-range transport of a single [[Small Craft]] of up to 200 tons. Like similar transport bays they provide landing, launch, maintenance and accommodations for a Small Craft, its crew and support personnel.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
<div class="gamerules">

==== Rules ====
Small Craft Bays take up one space slot, weigh 200 tons and have a capacity of 1 Small Craft of up to 200 tons per cubicle.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
</div>

=== Vehicle Bay ===
'''Vehicle Bays''' are provide the same basic facilities as 'Mech and Fighter bays, though they lack the same orbital launch, drop or recovery systems. Each cubicle is configurable for different sizes of vehicles, though they fall into the range classes of Light, Heavy and Super-Heavy. In a pinch a Super-Heavy Vehicle Bay can carry Small Craft.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
<div class="gamerules">

==== Rules ====
All Vehicle Bays take up one space slot and have a weight comparable to their maximum carry capacity weight. Light bays may carry a vehicle of up to 50 tons, Heavy bays up to 100 tons, and Super-Heavy bays up to 200 tons.<ref name="TMp239-240"/>
</div>

==Notes==
* It should be noted there have been two official [[errata]] updates since the publication of the original ''TechManual''. Errata changes done to the transport bay varies between the second update and third. Notable changes the inclusion of additional types of battle armor bays per size and tech origins (Clan and Inner Sphere) which varies in how many troopers the bay holds.
* Another official errata document published on 19 June 2024 clarified that any individual 'Mech, fighter, vehicle or small craft cubicle can only support a single such vehicle, but that bays can use the remaining mass capacity as standard cargo space if that vehicle is below the maximum mass; however, when used in this way the bays cannot be used for maintenance or combat drops.

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==
* ''[[TechManual]]''

## Weapon: Vehicular Dual Turret
### Data Extract Profile
{{Cleanup}}
==Description==
'''Vehicular Dual Turret''' (or '''Dual Turrets (Vehicular)''') is a piece of equipment that is available to both [[Combat Vehicle|ground]] and [[Combat_Vehicles#Naval_Vessels|naval]] combat & [[Support Vehicles|support vehicles]]. Dual turrets allow designers to set up a vehicle with multiple weapons mounted in two separate locations.

The two turret configuration is mounted to the forward and to the rear of the vehicle, which is designated as Forward Turret (FT) and Rear Turret (RT), respectively.  As part of their construction, the forward turret is unable to aim to the rear of the vehicle. The rear turret is set higher than the forward turret, giving the rear turret a 360° turning arc.<ref name="TOp347-348>"''Tactical Operations'', pp. 347-348, "Vehicular Dual Turret"</ref> 

Originally developed in [[BattleTech Eras|pre-spaceflight era]], the dual turret was considered [[experimental technology]] until [[3080]], when it would become commonly produced equipment for various Inner Sphere and Clan factions.  

<div class="gamerules">

==Game Rules==
Dual turrets are only available for [[Combat Vehicle|ground]] and [[Combat_Vehicles#Naval_Vessels|naval]] combat & [[support vehicles]]. Vehicles using this configuration of turret use the standard vehicle rules regarding turrets, except that the forward turret cannot fire in the rear arc. The rear turret is able to fire in any arc the player chooses. Both turrets are considered to be separate hit locations and have their own separate armor and internal structure locations. 

When receiving a hit to a turret location, the attacker must roll 1D6 and apply –2 modifiers for attacks from the forward arc. Turret hits received from attacks to the vehicle's rear arc apply a +2 modifier instead. This roll is to determine which turret is struck by the shot. A roll with modifier of 3 or less results in the forward turret being hit, while 4 or higher hits the rear turret due to elevated location. Any critical roll will be applied to the turret and its equipment assigned to it.<ref name="TOp347-348/>
</div>

== Equipment Gallery ==
<gallery>
Vidar TROPrototypes.jpg|[[Vidar]] Heavy Defense Tank featuring dual turrets

## Weapon: Viral Jammer
### Data Extract Profile
The '''RISC Viral Jammer''' is an experimental electronic warfare equipment created by the [[Republic of the Sphere]].

== Description  ==
[[Republic Institute of Strategic Combat | RISC's]] '''viral jammers''' might have been the ultimate weapon in electronic warfare, could they be made more stable and reliable in the field. Effectively devised as a kind of smart "hacking" software - at least distantly related to the first Star League's [[Centurion Weapon System]] - these systems produce a massive barrage of almost-randomly generated code that could interfere with hostile sensors and networking systems. The effect could overload enemy systems with indecipherable "junk data", creating a far more persistent effect than [[ECM Suite | standard ECM fields]] alone.

The transmitter system and the purpose-built computers used to make these viral jammers work, however, proved to be volatile. With 
[[Targeting-tracking system | modern sensor arrays]] and electronics warfare equipment fairly well hardened against external interference, these jammers had to be purpose built to defeat particular electronics system types, and burned out quickly in the effort to overpower these systems' inherent safeguards against interference.

This was particularly exacerbated by the fact that viral jammers were literally "fire and forget" technology, incapable of shutting down once engaged, and so aggressive in countering foreign signals that they could potentially interfere with friendly and hostile EW systems alike. Although RISC's specialists were reportedly able to program the jammers to synchronize with friendly electronics, improper coordination protocols or a flaw in the jammers design often saw the system cause havoc to friend and foe alike.

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==
* [[Interstellar Operations: Alternate Eras]], Pg. 88-89

## Weapon: Void-Signature System
### Data Extract Profile

The '''Void-Signature System''' is a hybrid evolution of [[Star League]] era systems such as the [[Chameleon light polarization shield]] and the [[null-signature system]]. 
==Overview==
Essentially a scaled up version of the [[Word of Blake|Word of Blake's]] [[mimetic armor]],<ref name="TOp349">''Tactical Operations'', p. 349, "Void Signature System"</ref> the void-signature system shields a [[BattleMech]]'s presence from both visual and electronic detection with help from a paired [[ECM suite]].
[[file:void-example.png|left|thumb|[[Raptor II]] with Void Signature System]]
Introduced by the Word of Blake in [[3070]] after ten years of research<ref name="TOp349"/> the void-signature system was rare. Though it was mounted on some unexpected units (including an ''[[Archer]]'' variant used by the Protectorate Militia), the void-signature system was usually seen on ''[[Raptor II]]'' variants and in the [[Shadow Division]]s. 

One limiting factor for the void-signature system was its high cost. The base system cost two million C-Bills, but that didn't include the cost of an ECM suite to power it.<ref name="TOp441">''Tactical Operations'', p. 411, "Heavy Weapons And Equipment Construction Data"</ref> This restricted the void-signature systems to those units the Blakist commanders thought could make the best use of it.

==Notes==
* Game rules for the use of this equipment could be found in '[[Tactical Operations]]''.<ref name=TOp349/>

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==

## Weapon: VTOL Jet Booster
### Data Extract Profile
==Description==
The '''VTOL Jet Booster''' is a piece of equipment used to increase the speed of any [[VTOL]]. The Booster itself has existed in different forms since the time of [[Early Space|Early Spaceflight]]. Its modern versions were separately created by [[Clan Hell's Horses]] in [[2839]], while [[Cal-Boeing of Dorwinion]] developed its own in [[3009]] in the [[Inner Sphere]].  The technology remained [[Experimental Technology|Experimental]] until the late [[Jihad]], when it entered limited production in [[3078]], and slightly later the equipment entered mass production.

== Rules ==
<div class="gamerules">
=== Game Rules ===
==== Gameplay ====
The VTOL Jet Booster doubles the cruise speed of any VTOL it is equipped to, but not its flank. While it does not need a special roll to be activated, when in use a piloting modifier of +3 is added in order for the vehicle to avoid making a sideslip. No special piloting maneuvers can be performed while the Booster is active.

==== Construction ====
The VTOL Jet Booster can be mounted on either a non-fixed-wing [[Combat Vehicle]] or [[Support Vehicle]]–type [[VTOL]].
The Booster is located in the main body of the unit and only one booster may be mounted per vehicle. The Booster's weight is 10 percent of a VTOL's engine weight, which is then rounded up to the nearest half ton, and the item takes up one slot. The booster cannot be Pod-mounted onto an [[OmniVehicle]].

Per Tactical Operation's construction rules table, the cost of a VTOL Jet Booster is 10,000 per x a Vehicle's Engine Rating.
The Equipment Rating for the VTOL Jet Booster, as of [[3075]], was D/X-F-E. However, this is no longer the case due to various equipment reclassifications, which began in 3078 and continue into the [[Dark Age era|Early Republic Era]].
</div>

==References==

## Weapon: VTOL Mast Mount
### Data Extract Profile
[[File:VTOL Mast Mount.jpg|thumb|right|150px|Image of a VTOL Mast Mount]]

The '''VTOL Mast Mount''' is a piece of equipment designed to  allow [[VTOL|VTOLs]] enhance its sensor abilities while allowing to protect craft from enemy fire.

==Description==
Introduced in the [[BattleTech Eras|Pre-Spaceflight Era]], the Mast Mount is mounted on the top of a VTOL's rotors.  A VTOL using a Mast Mount can use cover, and be able to see over the cover, while protecting itself from enemy fire. Mast Mounts were generally not found on modern VTOLs due the reduced size of them as compared to their pre-spaceflight predecessors.<ref name="TOp350">''Tactical Operations'', p. 350, "VTOL Mast Mount"</ref>

==Notes==
* Rules for the VTOL Mast Mount can be found in ''[[Tactical Operations: Advanced Units & Equipment]]''.

==References==
<references/>

==Bibliography==
* ''[[Tactical Operations]]''

## Weapon: Watchdog Combined Electronic Warfare System
### Data Extract Profile
Though not without limitations, the '''Watchdog Combined Electronic Warfare System''' (also known as '''Watchdog CEWS''' or the '''Watchdog System''') is a [[Clan]] developed component that combines the abilities of the [[Light Active Probe]] and [[ECM Suite]] in a single compact package.<ref name=THBp66>''Tactical Handbook'', p. 66, "Watchdog System"</ref> <ref name=TOp405>''Tactical Operations'', p. 405, "Heavy Weapons and Equipment Construction Data"</ref> <ref name=TOp278>''Tactical Operations'', p. 278, "Watchdog Composite Electronic Warfare System (CEWS)"</ref>

==Description==
In the years immediately prior to [[Battle of Huntress (3060)|their Annhilation]], [[Clan Smoke Jaguar]] feverishly initiated a number of new research & development programs seeking to both reinforce its [[Clan Smoke Jaguar Touman|''touman'']] and negate the few tactical advantages the [[Inner Sphere]] held over Clan technology - the [[C3 Network|C3 system]]. Starting in [[3057]], the Smoke Jaguar [[Scientist Caste]] would focus on the area of electronic warfare with what was then simply called the  "Composite Electronic Warfare System", with Inner Sphere intelligence agencies dubbing it the "Watchdog System", a combination of the Clan [[Active Probe]] and ECM Suite in single system, allowing a given Clan unit to devote the weight saved toward additional weaponry or armor. In comparison to full size Active Probes and ECM Suites, let alone the more less effective [[Beagle Active Probe]] or [[Guardian ECM Suite]]  the resulting Watchdog boasts a somewhat reduced range unsuitable for more dedicated recon units, but would provide powerful ECM and sensor abilities to more generalized combat forces to prevent ambush tactics.<ref name=THBp66/> <ref name=TOp278/>

Reaching the prototype design and production stage in [[3059]], the demise of the Jaguars would see the completed prototypes scattered among the various Clans as they continued to experiment with the technology.<ref name=TOp278/> In the wake of the [[Word of Blake]] [[Jihad]], when the value of ECM to combat the Blakists widespread use of [[improved C3 Computer]]s was amply proven, manufacture of the Watchdog CEWS would result in its appearance in both [[BattleMech]]s and [[OmniMech]] configurations by the [[Dark Age era]].

==References==
<references/>

* ''[[Tactical Handbook]]''
* ''[[Tactical Operations]]''
